ParlaMint-DK_20170202184358	Thank you very much. Thank you for those, I think, relevant and interesting comments on the proposal. I think that some of the points reflect a pre-sceptic attitude towards a broad, sensible settlement, in which one chooses to see the negative aspects of it instead of focusing on the great opportunities this offers for development in all parts of Denmark, while at the same time managing to maintain a reasonable balance. For the whole purpose of the review here and the amendment of the law of plans is to create better opportunities for growth and development throughout Denmark, while at the same time protecting nature and a good environment. The starting point is the agreement of the government that was made by the then government, the V government, with the Social Democratic Party, the KF and DF, on a better balance sheet for Denmark from June 2016. The law of plans helps to set the framework for development opportunities throughout Denmark, but we must note that the law of plans has not followed time in all areas, and there are many examples today that the law of plans creates challenges in relation to the initiative and commitment of companies and citizens, who are supposed to create growth and development locally. That is why a broad majority in the Folketing has now agreed on modernisation, which we have discussed this afternoon, in which we adjust the law in a number of areas, so that it will, I think, support potentials and improve opportunities for growth and development in all parts of Denmark. After all, we are removing growth barriers in spatial planning, and we are opening up new opportunities for development in, among others. rural areas in the tourism industry and also in the retail trade. And this is happening in a way where municipalities, companies and citizens will have better opportunities to create growth and development together, and, not least, where we are and this is also important to me as Minister ■ protects nature and the environment. I will give you a few examples of this, because we have heard some other examples in the debate here, and then I think it is important to tell you just what the real world is like in this proposal. The draft law contains a number of new opportunities that support urban growth and development. With the bill, we give greater opportunity to exploit buildings in the land zone that are unused to it may be a abandoned farm, a former school or a closed dairy. So there will now be an opportunity to settle and set up a business in what has been redundant buildings without having to apply for permission first. We are also making the framework for agriculture more up-to-date. There will be better opportunities to build operating buildings, as well as the possibility of visiting facilities and the like. At the same time, the draft law means that the municipalities must take more account of the development potential of the production companies by defining in future areas of activity reserved for production companies. Then cottage owners, and I think this is welcome, will have the opportunity to use their holiday home for a greater part of the year, and retired holiday home owners will have the right to live in their summer house for a whole year, even after they have lived there for one year. I think that is an opportunity that many pensioners will be happy about. The draft law also provides a number of new development opportunities in coastal areas. This means that municipalities will now be able to identify development areas where there is greater access to urban development and to build new facilities. In order to provide better conditions for tourism, it is also possible for municipalities to set up and reposition coastal cottage areas. Then there has been some discussion about the retail trade. I believe that what we should be trying to do by the Danish Parliament is to create a reasonable framework for the retail trade to be able to take care of itself. And I think that's what we've created with this new plan law. We would like productivity to be strengthened and we would also like to offer a greater supply of goods and lower prices. Against this background, therefore, we have opened the door to major shops and we have also removed the size restrictions for committee stores. It is complemented by new opportunities to establish and expand relief areas in cities across the country where there is a sufficient customer base. But we have also ensured, and I think it is important that it is not just the wild West that there are some rules that ensure that we still have shops in all parts of Denmark. And I think that diversity is important; that pluralism is important in relation to the whole area, so that citizens in all parts of Denmark have access to retail trade. Finally, the draft law provides for a more flexible and digital administration and allows municipalities to make better use of resources in spatial planning through new planning tools. Among other things: a new possibility of derogation from local plans is introduced for the temporary use of land, for example: temporary study accommodation which can help to alleviate the lack of study accommodation. We reached a 10-year derogation, as proposed by most of the Danish Parliament's parties, starting with the Danish People's Party. This is included in the bill here, which allows students to obtain study accommodation more easily in the study cities. At the same time, we shorten the consultation period for less intrusive plans and introduce a new, simplified local planning tool for minor changes to local plans. This means administrative simplification of the planning process in the municipalities so that citizens and businesses do not have to wait for a very long time for minor changes, but it is on the clear assumption that municipalities should continue to devote sufficient time to public participation, neighbouring consultation, etc. The democratic process remains in focus. In addition, the changes to more digital administration involve modernising legislation in the light of digital developments. So in view of it, I think, good debate that has been here now, I am sure that overall the bill will provide significantly better opportunities for growth, development and settlement throughout Denmark, while at the same time safeguarding a good environment and a rich nature. Thank you for your comments. I imagine that there will be a lot of questions when we have to deal with this in committee, and I will of course be available in the debate that will come when the matter is dealt with in the Committee on Industry.
